ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U35	Let us start a debate. The name of the Member, New York.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U36	Good morning, colleagues! I would like to draw your attention to the following. This draft law seems necessary and important, as it aims to reduce the shadow economy and create an account register where each citizen's accounts will be registered. This register will be maintained by the State Revenue Service. But, see what's going on. The norms on reporting to the account registry shall enter into force only on 1 July 2017. And when we open an annotation and see how the budget will benefit for this time, we will see that it is written that in 2017 it is planned to collect EUR 2 million 70 thousand for six months, because the norms enter into force on 1 July, and in 2018 - only EUR 2 million 230 thousand for 12 months. The natural question arises: Why will we collect only 10 percent more money during a period of six months? Why is that the prognosis? When we contacted the Ministry of Finance, of course, they claimed that the fundamental criteria, which have been used in this forecast and recorded in the annotation, are very important. What they are, they could not say. Consequently, it seems that this is something of a divination method, and the entire budget is also designed. In general, the idea is correct, but unfortunately the mathematics that is included there is not understandable. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U37	Thank you. I closed the debate. Will the rapporteur add a note on behalf of the Commission?

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U43	Let us start a debate. Name of Member Inguna Silver.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U44	Good morning, colleagues! I would first like to draw your attention to the quality of the draft laws we receive, and this is one of these. The draft law contains only eight articles, but for the second reading there are 86 proposals. In fact, the draft law is fully rewritten for the second reading. And my proposal is on this subject. If you looked at the argument, you noticed that this draft law takes over directives that have not yet been approved by the Council of Europe. The directives are still being negotiated, but the rules for transposing them are already being incorporated here. For example, here are definitions that do not yet exist in the law regarding crime prevention. So this bill tries to regulate norms that are still under discussion, which require both a broader discussion, interpretation and legislative reflection, which concerns all the activities related to crime prevention - both money laundering and the fight against terrorism. My proposal in this section of the definition is therefore a very important matter: so that this draft law does not begin to regulate what the directives have not yet been prepared for, and to regulate only this technical, or perhaps very important, case of the creation of a register of accounts itself, in so far as it concerns the directive requiring the creation of a register of such accounts. I therefore call for my proposal to be supported - not to speak in this draft law about authorised persons (which were, however, excluded) or about the beneficiary of the real good within the meaning of the future directives, which will only have to be taken over, because they are now - I say it again! - the Council of Europe has not yet been adopted. Please support my proposal. Thank you.

On behalf of the Commission on Budget and Finance (Tax) - Member Vilnis Ķirsis.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U322	Colleagues! A total of 10 proposals have been received. 1. - The Cabinet's proposal. It's supported.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U323	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Ivars Zarins.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U324	Good morning, colleagues! First let everyone wish a productive working day today. And I'll get to the point. This proposal is the whole salt of this draft law. It is therefore a change in the application of the mandatory procurement component. I will read to you the same thing. ‘ The costs, consisting of charges for installed electrical capacity, shall be borne by all end-users of Latvian electricity and shall be distributed among groups of voltage and consumption levels in proportion to the fixed part of the revenue from the system services by compensating the public trader for the purchase costs.’ Colleagues, I am convinced that almost none of you, who will be ready to support this proposal today, have any meaning at all that has been written here. You will not even be able to repeat it, but you will vote on it. However, I would like to state the substance of this proposal. Is There a Being? If, indeed, we want, as is publicly presented, to end up addressing the waste of the compulsory procurement component, then this is absolutely not done with the amendment of this draft law. Just what is being achieved? From one shoulder to another, this severity is hardly transferred to the other shoulders. However, this issue is not, by its very nature, addressed. We are told that these amendments will help to re-emerge the manufacturing industry. In fact, the situation may turn out to be very different. There are actually some companies that can really help with these amendments, and these are the big industrial companies that we really have and work well for. And it will help them. For the rest of the manufacturing industry, for those entrepreneurs who are emerging and who really want to develop, this proposal may have exactly the opposite effect. Of course, I am very pleased that we can help some large companies, and I am really happy that the Ministry of Economics has begun to do something in this direction. But the question is, should this be done at the expense of other manufacturing companies? This is certainly not the best solution. It is therefore completely absurd that, today, in this legislative package, we are looking at this matter as a matter of urgency, without proper discussion, because this proposal could have been significantly improved in principle. If the Ministry of Economics has at last had the courage and the determination to deal with this issue, we could do it in a neat, orderly manner, and really make sure that this issue really helps to develop for the whole economic sector. Instead, the urgency of the electricity market in general will once again be addressed in this way without any sense. It is very regrettable and, in my opinion, very wrong. I would call for this practice not to be pursued.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U325	The name is Arvil Asheraden, Minister for Economic Affairs.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U326	Dear Saeima! As an economic minister, I am truly grateful that the Saeima has looked at this issue, is moving forward and we have reached second reading: we are close to this issue. We are dealing with one extremely important problem with this draft law. In Latvia's economy, the energy solutions they have taken up so far have led to a situation in which electricity prices for end-users are the third most expensive in the European Union. As a result, we see how the Latvian manufacturing industry has been affected. Latvia's manufacturing share in the economy is 12 percent. In Estonia, which has chosen not industrial development but innovation development, it is 15 percent, in Lithuania it represents almost 20 percent of the economy. And the impact of electricity prices in this area is very impressive. If we are talking about the large Latvian energy-intensive manufacturing companies, which will cost more than 10 percent of their prices, and given that these prices are not competitive, it is quite clear that these companies will not be able to compete on global markets. If we are talking about “Liepājas metalurgs”, it is a question of electricity prices. If we are talking about Brocene Cemex, it is a question of electricity prices. If we are talking about the Valmiera glass fibre company, it is once again an issue of electricity prices. Well, here, and that is why the Ministry of Economics has put it and the government has supported a model where these OIC consumers are segmented by consumption, offering a solution to reduce the cost of OICs for major consumers and for smaller consumers. This model is not unique. This model is available in a number of Central European countries. In Germany, this segmenting model envisages that households also contribute to covering these costs of the compulsory purchase of renewable energy. Latvia has chosen an Austrian model, where these costs are covered in a segment between companies with different consumption volumes. If you approve these changes today, from 2018 Latvian energy-intensive companies will be able to receive electricity at a price equivalent to Central European prices. And when we discussed this issue with Cemex, which is actually working under quite difficult conditions due to price costs, they said quite clearly: if this price model enters into force in 2018, they will be able to offer equivalent products to German producers on the Scandinavian market. I would like to say that this is just one example of how we have acted to support our processing industry. If we do so, the manufacturing industry will increase its production volumes instead of looking at how to fight here and whether to move its production facilities to other countries, and I think within two or three years we will see a substantial increase in the manufacturing industry. Thank you.

In the past, even at the time the Law on Budget and Financial Management was written, there was a division that the relevant minister determined a lot of his orders or regulations in the sector. The principle that this responsibility has essentially shifted to the Cabinet of Ministers now also determines the content that the Law on Budgets and Financial Management should contain. This norm, which has existed since the beginning of the 1990s - that the Minister of Finance determines precisely the organisation and management of the budget implementation process - was established with the idea that all ministries should be bound by the instructions issued by the Treasury. However, as I said, there is no such regulation anymore. But logically, there is a common responsibility in life - the Minister of Finance is responsible not only for the organisation and management of the budget, but also for a very important part... This is the answer, starting with budget planning, with how not only the implementation of the state budget, but also the implementation of municipal budgets is ensured, as well as monitoring the whole process. Therefore, our proposal is to combine together and show all responsibility, starting with the planning of the budgetary process; to show how the budgetary process is managed, implemented and also how it is accounted for. Please support this proposal.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U540	Dear colleagues, The Association of the Regions of Latvia submitted a proposal to supplement Article 25 of the law with a new - fourth - part as follows: surviving spouses have the right to receive an old-age pension of the deceased spouse, with a further waiver of their old-age pension. Unfortunately, in the Commission, this proposal was not moved further, with formal reasons. In our neighboring Lithuania, since 1995, there have been so-called widows' pensions. They have historically changed from 50 per cent of the deceased's spouse's pension to the current rate of flat-rate benefits at the surviving pension. You are well aware that the current pension system in Latvia determines that every citizen creates his/her own pension capital. The Latvian Association of Regions believes that this should be changed in a socially equitable way, because there is a different distribution of social roles within the family: one is a deserver and the other is a name holder. Pensions are therefore also different. But living together is enough. When the earner dies, the second pensioner is now in extreme poverty, so the widow's pension would be an important step towards improving the day-to-day and survival conditions of pensioners who have become widows. Let's take action, colleagues, in accordance with the Constitution of the Republic of Latvia and its preamble spirit, helping old and single widows pensioners, and in the Law “On State Pensions” please strengthen the norm that the surviving spouse has the right to receive the largest pension of the deceased spouse by giving up his/her own pension. Please support this proposal.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U638	Currently, the law already provides that the municipality may participate in the financing of private educational institutions on the basis of a reciprocal agreement. This is not an obligation, but municipalities with an interest in improving quality are also supported by private schools. My proposal is based on the operation of the Preili Free School and the problems faced by this private school. As far as I know, it is the only educational institution of this type, located more than 100 kilometres from Riga. The local government, on the one hand, seems to be interested in improving the quality of education, but at the same time, of course, sees a certain threat to the existing school network maintained by the municipality. However, in this case, ladies and gentlemen, the losers are parents - those who, when they see the reality of the state- provided education, come up with their own initiative, set up an association, invest their own financial resources and volunteer work in order to provide children with education that is right today, but do not want to leave their place of residence in the region. Parents themselves go together on school holidays, engage in quality monitoring and, as far as possible, in the learning process. In fact, the biggest losers will ultimately be these children because they are denied access to better education. Many parents choose private schools, not because of the prestige, but because of the alternative education methods available there, which are largely not used in schools founded by municipalities. Concerns often arise: if we allow parents to choose, local schools will reduce the number of pupils. But is that really a threat? Perhaps it is, I would even say, an opportunity for schools founded by municipalities not to lose competition and try to improve their supply. Parents choose alternative schools because changes take place too slowly in schools founded by municipalities. For those parents who have dared to act themselves, it would probably be easier to leave their home town, move closer to Riga and find one of the educational institutions that would be more suitable and supported by local government. But does the state want to encourage the evasiveness of such people, in this case, of the fundamentals of society - families with children - from the region? I would like to emphasise that it would be important for us to distinguish between at least two types of private schools: the schools that I have just mentioned, and the so-called richer schools, which offer some exclusive training programmes, and which pay 10 000 euros and even more per year. Colleagues, already today, according to government regulations, municipal money for mutual payments for pupils is also being followed by another municipality. However, if the parents choose a private school, the municipality may pay nothing for the student’s stay. The Ombudsman supports parents and points out that the municipality should co-finance private schools, as this would provide more complete opportunities for parents to choose freely where to teach their child. In addition, the existing arrangements constitute an infringement of the principle of equality. The Ombudsman already issued such an opinion a year ago, inviting the Ministry to remedy the deficiencies identified. However, within a year, the Ministry has not even begun to comply with the Ombudsman’s request. Obviously, loyalty issues are more important, and you will see in the next proposals that they are pushed through, so to speak. Although they have no budgetary implications and were rejected by the Education, Science and Culture Commission. But the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission seems to know better about the subtleties of loyalty. Ladies and gentlemen! This proposal, which I tabled, must be read in conjunction with proposals 4, 33 and 44. It is the 33st proposal that state and local governments participate in financing the remuneration of teachers of private educational institutions in accordance with the procedure established by the Cabinet of Ministers. According to the proposal 44, we would allow the Cabinet of Ministers to develop this procedure by 1 August next year to support those private schools offering alternative methods of education, and especially those operating further from Riga. Of course, municipalities should not be forced to finance those private schools that are just a business project. But if there are some selfish jobs in the system, we must not destroy all the hope of access to modern education for their sakes. I invite you to trust at least so much in the Cabinet of Ministers, because, as I said, in my proposal I am calling on you to discuss this procedure precisely in the Cabinet of Ministers regulations, and I hope that the Cabinet of Ministers could really do this, namely, with a few suggestions, to eliminate these risks that I have mentioned about business projects in private schools. In turn, if you do not support my proposals, you will show that you do not trust the Cabinet of Ministers, nor do you want to support progressive changes in educational institutions of our country based on fair funding principles. I call for support for this proposal 3, as well as for proposals 4, 33 and 44 which I have tabled. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U659	Let us start a debate. The name of the Member, New York.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U660	Colleagues! The proposed framework for so-called loyalty amendments is nothing new. As early as 1972, the Chancellor of West Germany, Willie Brant, introduced a so-called anti-radical act, namely a professional ban. This ban meant that a person applying to work in the public sector had to show his trust in democracy and in the rules of law. What these amendments provide for is a step in that direction. It is not clear why we need to change loyalty once again, because the amendments adopted in 2015 already provided that loyalty to the Republic of Latvia and the Constitution are one of the prerequisites for working as a teacher. Therefore, it cannot be said that this issue is not regulated by law. According to the data of the State Service of Education Quality, since 2015 there have been at least two cases where these amendments are appropriate for life and teachers or school directors have been punished according to the procedure established by law. Therefore, we cannot claim that they do not work because IKVD initiates a verification case and assesses the situation when receiving information about a possible violation of the law. Nor is the question of the fact that the law does not apply, that the authorities concerned do not have the tools to act when they receive information from the competent authorities or from any other authorities. All the more! I believe that these amendments cannot be part of the budget package and cannot be seen in urgent procedure, in two readings, because as early as in 2011, the Constitutional Court established in its judgment that the legislator has the right and at the same time the obligation to include only issues relating to a particular economic year in the package of the Law on the State Budget and the laws accompanying it. It is difficult to figure out how these amendments relate to a specific economic year and how we save funds in the budget by adopting these amendments. So I call for support for Amendment 9 and for loyalty issues to be excluded from the budget package. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U661	The name of Member Ilze Viņķelis.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U662	Colleagues! When these amendments, the so-called changes of loyalty, started his way to the Saeima, philosopher Ilmārs Šlīkins wrote: “There is no longer any power to talk to power, but I believe that today’s amendments to the Education Law are unlovable to Latvia.” We have no advantage in talking to power in this room. It is our job, it is our duty. Our duty is to create quality laws that deal with problems that unite Latvian society and not divide it. In my opinion, the draft law in its original performance is a strong evidence of disrespect and distrust towards the public, especially the educators and the Saeima. And I'll see why. The draft law was not discussed. The problem of the draft law was not first and foremost discussed in the teaching community. Here again there are attempts to polarise that discussion and say, “If you are against us, then you are with Putin!” This is a simplification of the discourse, and it is a recognition of its weakness. We are strong enough independent in Latvia to be able to discuss the most complex issues with society openly. The second argument that led the Commission to decide to ask for these amendments to be excluded from the bill: it was a legal parade. ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U664	Dear colleagues, The Budget and Finance (Tax) Commission supported several draft laws relating to the public budget for next year at final reading, including those which previously led to a wide public debate. In its second final reading, the Commission also supported amendments to the Education Law, specifying the cases in which state-owned educators may be dismissed from work. The amendments caused reasonable displeasure among the industry. These norms of the law are to be excluded from the draft laws accompanying the budget.The Latvian Education to Scientific Workers' Union, the Saeima Education, Culture and Science Commission, the Group SASKAŅA, the Latvian Municipalities Union, as well as the Saeima Legal Office are invited and invited. Initially, the draft law stipulated that, in cases where the teacher has not complied with the obligation to create an educational attitude towards others, work, nature, culture, society and the state, to raise honest, honest, responsible people, patriots of Latvia, the employer must immediately terminate the employment legal relationship with the educator. At the same time, the employer was required to terminate the employment legal relationship with the teacher or head of the educational institution immediately if he is not loyal to the Republic of Latvia and the Constitution. In view of the objections raised, the Cabinet of Ministers presented new proposals. The majority of the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission supported these proposals. For example, a decision was taken to supplement the first sentence of the fourth paragraph of Article 30 with the words “which is loyal to the Republic of Latvia and its Constitution” with the words “including non-discrimination and differential treatment of persons”. The proposals submitted by the Cabinet of Ministers and supported by the Commission provide that if a teacher or the head of an educational institution is not loyal to the Republic of Latvia and the Constitution and has not complied with the above-mentioned obligation, and if this is established by the State Education Quality Service or the employer, a person shall be prohibited from taking up the position of head of an educational institution or a teacher. Among other things, the Saeima's Education, Culture and Science Commission has not even looked at these proposals. If you approach this thing philosophically, then it also happens that dismissal itself in this case is a discrimination against the expression of any particular position, behaviour or belief. This is very bad and probably illegal, as one of the most topical issues of the right to work today is the prevention of discrimination in labour law, employment conditions in terms of access to employment, pay and other aspects. In this sense, the right to work must be attributed to the equality established in Article 91 of the Satversme before the law and the court, as well as the prohibition of discrimination. I do not exclude that this issue may reach the Constitutional Court. As the President of the Constitutional Court Aldis Laviņš pointed out in an interview with the LNT programme “900 Seconds”: “The existence of such regulations, in my opinion, quite clearly indicates that there is a restriction of Article 106 of the Satversme, which establishes the right to employment. But I want to emphasise that in a democratic and legal state, fundamental rights can be restricted if they have a legitimate, societal objective." ( End quoted) “This question can be discussed,” he added. I would like to remind you that the first sentence of Article 106 of the Satversme, "Everyone has the right to choose freely according to their abilities and qualifications", applies to the right of a person to work. This article is to be considered as a ‘roof article’, which defines the fundamental issues of the right to work, taking into account the whole set of abilities, knowledge and skills that characterise the individual’s readiness and suitability for certain work. Any limitation of rights must be assessed without delay in the context of each particular case on the basis of the principle of proportionality. I therefore call for the specific amendments to be dealt with in the usual order and not in two readings. I call for the exclusion of Article 2 of the draft law. Thank you.

Will it be easier for the country to put another teacher in each such class, who will follow this one child in particular, who cannot learn with all the others? Because that is what the reform is intended to do. These children are now isolated in a special isolated environment, but then they will be in the environment with all the other children. Therefore, I call on you to listen to our proposal and to prevent them from destroying the environment that is currently in boarding schools, which allows these children, more than two thousand children, to find their place in life. And most importantly, respect those educators who for many years have acquired special skills, special skills and skills to work with such children! They work with these children in classes where there are no more than nine or 12 children, as this is the maximum to manage the education process at all. And again, my very dear call. The budgetary process will end, Christmas holidays will start, and then there will be a lot more time left. Well, find the time and go to some boarding school! It is worth changing your attitude towards society and understanding how many people live in society today. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U778	Members are in favour.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U780	Let us start a debate. The name of the Member, New York.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U781	Colleagues! This draft law has as much to do with the budget as it was with the previous one, namely nothing. Well, if you don't take into account the fact that there are means to set up some kind of register. So what is the draft law supposed to do? Introduce a register of students and graduates. With the students, everything is clear. It has long been discussed and supported. But on the register of graduates there are a whole range of questions to which answers cannot be found either in the draft law or in the annotation. In the Commission, the first proposal we received from the Legal Office of the Saeima was to exclude this draft law from the budget package. Why? Because this has no obvious impact on next year's national budget, and even more so! - there are no answers to a whole series of questions, which will allegedly be answered in the Cabinet of Ministers regulations. But the proposed design differs significantly from the rules already governing other registers, such as university registers, academic staff registers and diploma registers. The legislator has listed in detail the particulars to be entered in the registers, the procedure for submitting them and other circumstances relevant to the operation of the register. There's nothing like that this time. We do not know what the purpose is, what data will be requested, how they will be imported into these registers, how personal data protection problems will be dealt with and so on, and so on. The register of graduates is very much needed both for universities and for the ministry. But the answers to these questions should be found before we adopt this law, so that there is clarity for all players - universities, graduates and students alike. I call on you not to support the creation of this register.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U782	Name to Member Juris Villums.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U783	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Nikiforov has already mentioned that this draft law does not really relate to the budget. The Legal Bureau also pointed out this, and its manager Dina Meistere also sent a letter to us to the Commission. I'm not going to repeat that. I would add that the Board of Directors joined these objections by the Legal Bureau. In the letter of the Board of Directors it was indicated that the universities whose application of the draft law would be most direct were not involved in the drafting and coordination of the draft law. The university conceptually supports the establishment of the register, however, it cannot support the current version of the draft law until the technical solutions for implementing the register of students are clear. And I will also mention the costs, because at first, the argument that there would be any costs for public or private universities was not mentioned at all. Now, at second reading, this situation has been slightly corrected... as far as I understand it has been improved, but I still have no confidence that sufficient financial resources will be provided in accordance with the procedure laid down by the Cabinet of Ministers. I am afraid that universities will also have to find financial resources in their own already limited resources, so that these additional functions can be fulfilled. The Council for Higher Education also added to the concerns I had just expressed. As I said, all of this was pointed out by the Latvian Council of Rectors. And I can add that, in my opinion, such a register - a register of students and graduates - whose main aim is to find out how extensively employed and how well-paid students and graduates of each particular university are, shows, in essence, that the government (and thus the Ministry) will continue to feel around the field of education, but does not know how to do it itself. This register will, of course, show what we already know well, including the fact that the graduates of regional universities are rarely the beneficiaries of their highest salaries. I would remind you that even within one state-owned company, for example, within the State Revenue Service, employees receive different remuneration for performing similar or even identical duties in Riga and abroad. It is also more difficult to start your own business in the region than in Riga or close to Riga. This is one of the reasons why there are fewer companies in the regions. This is less so not because entrepreneurs are more lazy or otherwise unable to operate there, but simply because the situation is such that there are no conditions for equal and fair competition in those regions. In conclusion, the aim of this draft law is not understood, there is no clear expectation of results and benefits. I therefore call for the vote to be taken against this proposal and against the whole of the draft law.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U840	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U841	Dear colleagues, My proposal, Amendment 6, can be seen in conjunction with Proposal No 7 and Amendment 13, so I will allow all three to report at once. The tax system now has a strong fiscal role, but the aim is to take measures to make the tax system work for economic development. And the purpose of our proposals is to motivate companies to invest their profits in assets, to accumulate for new projects, so we propose to tax their profits only on the part of the profits distributed in dividends but not on the undistributed profits - not to tax them. How is corporate tax currently being paid? The company shall draw up a corporate income tax return, submit it to the State Revenue Service, further calculate the tax according to the declaration and transfer the tax to the state budget. We propose to introduce a duty suspension regime. As before, the company will draw up a corporate income tax return, submit it to the State Revenue Service, calculate the taxable income, but the tax on this taxable income will be paid at the current 15 per cent rate only after the company members take a decision on the distribution of profits into dividends and not after the declaration has been submitted to the State Revenue Service. As a result, taxable income will in future be able to accumulate in undistributed dividends, as companies may currently accumulate losses for years. If dividends split part of the current year and profits of previous years, then the calculated tax is paid in the state budget in proportion to the profit share that is distributed into dividends. The application of the deferred tax regime is an option. Those who want to pay the tax immediately will be able to continue to do so. However, those who would still be in the interest of pooling funds for future development will not be able to do so. The law will send a clear signal to entrepreneurs: “Collect profit! No one will take it away from you!” And that will mean that there will no longer be any need to hide the amount of profits from the tax administration. This is a very important event, because so far we do not even know and there is no clear idea of how much profit is made by a joint enterprise in the Republic of Latvia. There is currently a system of flat-rate taxation (that is to say, a court) allowing the nominal rate of 15 per cent to be reduced to an effective rate of 6.4 per cent. But the administrative performance of the discount system is very complex, it may be difficult for small and medium-sized enterprises to learn it. Our proposal, in turn, is clear and simple. And by the end. Tax reform is extremely topical now, also because economic forecasts are increasingly pointing to a new economic crisis. And in order to protect society and the country from it, it is the last time we end up getting money out of the economy and creating, through the application of taxes, a powerful motivation for economic operators, which would crave them so strongly that all the locks to gambling and success and consistent economic competitiveness, which is the best protection against the crisis, would be opened up. The proposals are a step towards strengthening the economy. Please support them. Thank you.

By September, the proposals had to be prepared but failed to reach agreement on this in the government, because there were too many different and even controversial ideas from the business organisation, the Ministry of Economics and the Ministry of Finance. I think that the greatest resistance to entrepreneurs who are micro-enterprises is not due to this deadline, but due to the entry into force from 1 January of the legislative amendments adopted last year, which require minimum social contributions to be paid, or... in fact three quarters of the minimum social contributions. Why is this solution? Because according to the Ministry of Welfare, 70 thousand people in Latvia are socially insecure. And it is true that, in the case of a mother having to go on parental leave and in the case of sickness and unemployment, the benefit that a person can receive from the state is extremely small, because he is not paid enough social contributions. And that is why we have this solution, which was proposed last year. With the participation of business organisations, under the leadership of the Ministry of Welfare and the Ministry of Finance, it was proposed that the tax rate for micro-enterprises should be reduced from nine per five per cent while the minimum social contributions should be gradually introduced. The rules of the game are different. For people working under the general regime, their working time (and therefore their pay) is summed up, and it is more difficult for those working under the micro-enterprises tax regime. And if it was thought that micro-enterprises could also be able to sum up this time, then there are a number of arguments why this cannot be done. For example, there are even cases where one person works in 28 different micro-enterprises, which is practically impossible. Therefore, we understand that we need to resolve a number of things: to ensure the social protection of people and to offer a special tax regime for small businesses. ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1070	Dear Chairman of the Saeima. Dear colleagues, I would also like to talk about the other proposals of the Association of the Regions of Latvia in general. The Association of the Regions of Latvia calls for the planned amendments to the Law “On State Social Insurance”, which should enter into force on 1 January of next year, and which provide for the introduction of mandatory social insurance contributions from the full national minimum wage even if the worker has a lower salary, so if the employee is working part-time. I think there's no blasphemy. These changes to the law “On State Social Insurance” were adopted a year ago, and everyone has had the opportunity to react, plan and replan. I believe that part of the blame must be borne by us, politicians who have explained too little and justified the need for these changes and, in particular, their technical implementation. We can see that citizens, businesses and municipalities are dissatisfied with the foreseeable change in the law on compulsory social security contributions. And it is a serious signal that changes to the law in their current wording are not acceptable to society and are not ready for society. We received a letter of alarm from the voters - from both cities and regions, especially from rural areas - that justifies the fact that the amendments to the law do not coincide with the reality. So the Excel table box shows one number, but the reality is completely different. There is an additional burden - the tax burden on labour - on entrepreneurs, various non-governmental organisations and associations, and there is a lack of knowledge about the future of workers, too, because these changes will affect the rural region most. One example. We are all actively preparing for national centenary events, especially the Song Celebration, but if we adopt this draft law, it may happen that some of the artistic self-governing and folk art groups in rural areas could not participate in all of this, because they have simply ceased their activities. And why? Just for the reason. It is already common practice in the regions that several self-employed collectives are led by one person in several regions, each receiving part - time pay. In fact, full-time work is collected in a number of workplaces and not all employers will be able to cover social security contributions in full pay. And another example is public organisations, which employ several part-time employees to perform various public and socially important tasks, which do not require full-time work. With these changes, we risk increasing the number of the shadow economy, as well as the number of unemployed and beneficiaries of social benefits. Is this risk worth it at this point? I therefore call once again on us all to take these decisions responsibly together and to support the proposal of the Association of the Regions of Latvia to abolish the planned changes for one year, so that, at this time, there is still a broad discussion with workers, with employers and with the social partners, because, in my opinion, it is most important to clarify how these contributions will be made and counted technically. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1071	Name of Minister for Finance Dana Reizniece-Ozola.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1072	Thank you for your reproof, Mrs. Cleinberg. I think it is right to say that we have not done enough work to explain how the minimum social contributions will actually be paid in a situation where a person is employed in several jobs. We are talking about the general regime or non-governmental organisations, where the law allows the working time to be summed up. We will correct this error and we will carry out an active information campaign by the end of the year. Already now anyone who has no clear rules of the game is invited to call the State Revenue Service. The website of the State Revenue Service contains a telephone number where you can get consultations. How, though, will this work? If a person is employed in more than one place of employment and, by adding up these working hours, works with at least a minimum wage, the full minimum social contribution must be paid to the employer on whom the employee's tax return is located. If a person has not submitted the employer's tax return, this full minimum social contribution must be paid to the employer for whom the employee has worked the longest. Question: "How can an employer know that he is under this obligation?" The State Revenue Service will inform employers about their obligation to pay a minimum social contribution to employees in the EDS system at the beginning of January, January 1. At the same time, if an employee works in several workplaces and this time is cumulatively higher than the minimum social contribution, this means that this employer, who has paid the minimum social contribution, has paid the social contribution overpayment, and the State Revenue Service will compensate the employer for this overpayment. The mechanism is not simple, I agree. But in a situation where an employee works with several employers, the employer will not pay the tax overall.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1187	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1188	Ladies and gentlemen, This proposal, Proposal 6, can be seen in conjunction with Proposals 8, 11, 12, 14 and 17. I will report all six at once. They have to be talked about more using figures than words, and so it may be difficult to listen to them, but that is the draft law, and that is the description of tax activity. In order to apply personal income tax to income, the taxable income is divided, as we know, into two parts: EUR 75 is set at zero and the remainder at 23 per cent. And labour income or salary accounts for about 89 per cent of total income of the population. On the other hand, income from capital other than capital gains (and these are dividends, interest income on deposits and bonds, as well as income from contributions from private pension funds) is charged at a rate of 10 per cent. The return on capital, which is an increase in capital (and is the difference between the price of capital, the sale and the purchase price), is set at 15 percentage points. The substance of our proposal is as follows: Firstly, to impose all forms of income on citizens at the same rate. Secondly, to divide the taxable income into four parts: EUR 160 at all (i.e. zero rate), the next EUR 440 at 15 percentage points, the next EUR 900 at 25 percentage points and the remaining part of the taxable income at 35 percentage points. The rates therefore change to the following thresholds: EUR 160, EUR 600 and EUR 1500. Such taxation of personal income is called progressive. Progressive income tax is lower than the current personal income tax if the taxable income after the payment of the worker's compulsory social insurance contribution does not exceed EUR 1806. ( The drug has increased noise. The head of the sitting shall be knocked down by means of a hammer.) This means that, in the first half of this year, the average monthly salary, which was equal to EUR 836 per month, will be subject to the progressive income tax, which is now being seen here - the one we are proposing - which will be EUR 51 less than the current system is putting it. This money will remain in employees' money wallets. This means that they will have the opportunity to spend more. As a result, it will also stimulate, increase market demand and reduce costs for employers. Next, I would like to say that it is very important to bear in mind here that the burden has in any case been shifted from taxpayers with lower income to taxpayers with higher income. The model has been calculated on the basis of 2014 data, which we received from the State Revenue Service in order to ensure a neutral fiscal effect. And this system works and, as a result of this system, we have obtained the following results. So if this progressive tax were to be applied, the personal income tax payer... the progressive tax would reduce the tax on wages by less than eight percent, the tax on business income by almost half. On the other hand, the income from the increase in capital - it increases twice, and on the dividends it increases significantly - more than three times... Our main problem is social inequality. It reduces demand on the market. This leads to poverty for the majority of our population. In order to combat this system, the progressive tax is a tool that is being used in all the Member States of the European Union, with the exception of the Baltic, Romania, Hungary, Slovakia and the Czech Republic. I think that our path, however, leads to a system that operates on the west side of the European Union. I call for our proposal to be supported. Thank you.

Therefore, colleagues, I will not go into the essence of this law, I will not tell you how it is calculated, because, to be honest, it is quite difficult for a simple person to understand it, too. It mentions the years in which the car has been lost. Until 2005 or until 2009, a younger or older car, as the tax is calculated, will not be pumped through this system. Therefore, I would still remind you that, in my view, it would be simpler to abolish this tax in its entirety and to include these financial acquisitions in the tax, and therefore all those who would buy fuel - the more they would drive, the more they would pay. Consequently, we would obtain in the budget those funds that would be lost if we were to give up this tax.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1309	Colleagues, welcome for the second time! The amendments to the rates of vehicle operation tax can be seen in the joint package with the draft law already supported today, from which the Ministry of Transport called for a complete waiver. It's about the tax on cars and motorcycles... Recognition as invalid... When a vehicle or a motorcycle was first registered, the vehicle owners had to pay around 100 to several thousand euros. Consequently, the budget revenue chapter is no longer planned for this tax. In turn, changes to Article 4 of the draft law, which defines the vehicle taxable persons, foresee conceptual changes, giving up the three indicators applied so far: vehicle mass, power and engine capacity. In future, we only use one indicator: the industrially determined CO2 emissions indicator. Thus, cars that are younger than 31 December 2008 and are registered for the first time in Latvia will be subject to the new tax calculation principle from 1 January 2017. In turn, for those cars that are under 2009 but are already registered in Latvia, a transitional period is offered until January 1, 2019 so that if a vehicle has high CO2 emissions and enters a group with rising payments, it would have a reasonable transitional period to switch the vehicle to one with lower CO2 emissions. At the same time, I draw attention to the fact that vehicles older than 2009 retain the principle of calculation, that is to say, the three indicators mentioned above: vehicle mass, power and engine capacity. With this proposal, we are calling for vehicles with lower CO2 emissions, or emissions, and for a simpler calculation so that cars with small and medium CO2 emissions pay less, but those cars with higher or significantly higher CO2 emissions would pay more proportionately. On the other hand, cars with emissions of up to 50 grams per kilometre would not pay at all and thus reduce the negative impact of transport on our common nature. Thank you.

In this case, a notary who knows about the inherited mass will have to report to the State Revenue Service. Moreover, it is not a question of financing terrorism or money laundering! ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U1683	Dear colleagues, Dear colleagues, Start with the draft law “On the medium-term budgetary framework for 2017, 2018 and 2019” at second reading. First of all, some introductory words in relation to this draft law. Both the 2017 budget and the medium-term budgetary framework for 2017-2019 address health, education and safety issues as priority issues. On the draft laws themselves and on the proposals for funding submitted. 666 proposals were submitted to the second reading of the draft law “On the State Budget for 2017”, while 644 proposals were submitted to the State Budget Framework for 2017-2019. Of these, 642 proposals were submitted for reallocation of funding for each draft law. Of these proposals, 459 were submitted by Members and 183 were submitted by the Cabinet of Ministers. The Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission, examining the proposals put forward by Members of the Saeima for the allocation of funds to measures and investment projects, found that Members focused mainly on the purpose of the allocation, not always specifying the most appropriate budget line. For example, Members have put forward a number of proposals on the allocation of funding to churches, churches, where they foresee that it can be done through both the Ministry of Justice and the Ministry of Culture, or through municipalities. The situation is similar with the allocation of funding to different societies. In order to ensure a uniform approach, in line with the objective and scope of the grant, the Budget and Finance (tax) Commission of the Saeima commissioned the Ministry of Finance to assess each particular case and, following the vote of the Saeima, to put them in the budget in the most appropriate manner, by technically sorting them out through restaurants, programmes, sub-programmes or classification codes. Similarly, this issue has been addressed in previous years, and experience has shown that the use of this principle has so far had no problems with the management of funding. Proposals supported or not supported. The budget and financial (tax) commission of the Saeima supported 554 proposals in the 2017 budget, but did not support 80 proposals. In the examination of the proposals, the Budget and Finance (Tax) Commission followed the principle of fiscal responsibility, and we supported in the Commission the proposals for funding the various measures and investment projects for a total amount of around EUR 20 million, without increasing the established deficit level of 1.1% of gross domestic product. The funds for unforeseen events for 2017 are planned to amount to about 40 million, similar to that in the 2016 budget. These are provisional estimates, which will be clarified following the respective votes of the Saeima today. And now the transition to the draft law “On the medium-term budgetary framework for 2017, 2018 and 2019”. 1. - a proposal from the Group of the Association of the Regions of Latvia, which the Commission decided not to support.

So if today Members have made proposals that they want to see the Ministry of Culture as responsible for organising the events, then it will be that when the budget law is signed, these expenses will no longer appear in the Ministry's budget, but maybe they will have moved to the Ministry of Justice's budget or the Ministry of Welfare's budget or to the target grants for the municipality. I believe that if, to the extent that it happens in this budget, responsibility for expenditure is changed, it is not a technical clarification, it cannot be called a breakdown of expenditure according to the budget classification. Because the budget classification is not a transfer of expenditure from one ministry to another ministry's responsibility. I therefore consider that such provisions are not acceptable. What had to be done - at the time the motions were tabled, they had to be formulated immediately as alternative proposals from the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission, with the right location for these expenditures. I call for this type of action not to be supported. I consider such a permissiveness, which occurs after Members have voted, to be absolutely unacceptable. Please do not support this type of initiative!

They are not just books like these; they are accompanied by battle cards. Why am I telling you all this? Because these books are not just for reading. These discs are attached to them, where you can see interactively, for example, that Kalashnikov's machine is not an invention from Russia, but "spected" from Shmeiser. You can interactively put both machines together, break down and combine (this is very instructive for young people!) And then you can see that they are similar to two drops of water. And one more thing. The specialists of the Latvian War Museum usually work on such books, and all the forms of the Second World War, all types of weapons are like encyclopaedias, and I am ashamed that until now, while the Latvian National Warriors Society had not begun to deal with this matter, encyclopaedias about the Second World War were released in Moscow, which were on very luxurious paper and in good colours, which also had the history of Latvian soldiers - both the Legion of Latvia and the Corps of Latvian Stringmen - images with all weapons, with forms. So they have the opportunity to publish such books, we don't. Thus, we continue to support this Latvian National Warriors' Society, and I call on colleagues to support them, because it is a very happy work.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U2298	Dear colleagues, Recently, we supported Barren's Sunday with great enthusiasm - the President of Latvia organised the adoption. Speaking about the vital importance of orphans living in their families, the President said about the importance of supporting foster families and ensuring that children do not live in orphanages. What we are proposing is to turn these words into works. The Ministry of Welfare and, I hope, Mr Reira have also declared that the growth of orphans in families, deinstitutionalisation, the reduction in the number of children in children's homes and the professionalisation of foster families are priorities of the Ministry of Welfare. Colleagues, I ask for this to be demonstrated in my work, not just in words. At present, the problem with the residence of orphans in either the orphanage or the family is actually caused by the unsubstantiated financial flow. In particular, we pay EUR 700 per month from the national budget for the presence of each child in the child's home. On the other hand, we pay a little over 100 euros a month for having a child in a foster family. However, despite this, the study carried out by the family of fosters shows, firstly, that there are more than 80 free foster families in Latvia who would be ready to take children and, secondly, that by sorting out this cash flow and giving the fosters half of what is now devoted to orphanage, deinstitutionalisation has been carried out much more smoothly and children could find their families more quickly. Colleagues, this proposal also provides for savings in the budget, which is why I am asking for it to be supported by giving foster families for each child only half of the amount of the child's household allowance. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U2299	Name to Minister for Welfare Janis Reirs.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U2300	Thank you to my colleague Bite for updating this issue. I would like to inform the Saeima that 4.9 million children have been allocated to this budget. This amount is also available next year, and in 2019 EUR 8.9 million is provided for professionalising the institution of foster families. I would like to remind you that no child is subject to deinstitutionalisation. Deinstitutionalisation is a process of social care centres where children with special needs are mentioned; deinstitutionalisation in no way applies to municipal children's homes, which are municipal institutions. Therefore, please be precise in the wording and also in the proposals. Thank you. And please follow the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission proposals.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U2978	Let us start a debate. The name of Member Arvīds Platoper.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U2979	Mr. Krauze, very respectable! It would be very good if, two years ago, I said that I shouldn't bring this money into the Saeima for a single choir... Unfortunately, you didn't listen at that time. We divided two million, and it could still be understood. Now we're sharing 20 million! I feel like a market where every product is priced and every place has to pay money, and there's the corruption that can't be arranged in any way. I think the Saeima is exactly the same. Before I give an assessment of these... I will read to Mr Krauze what those who do not come to the Saeima elections - about 46 percent. ‘ There's no public money. You can forget that! It's just taxpayers' money. And the Members yelled for the taxpayers, so they earn the votes of the voters. Politicians misappropriate taxpayers' money as a personal person and distribute it completely inappropriately. There are so many acute needs in the country, but money is spent on pointless projects, given to private individuals. ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U4539	Dear colleagues, Please draw your attention to the issue raised by the municipality of Ludza in the context of the next year's budget review. This is about financial support from the state budget for the reconstruction of the bridge over the Garbar River located in Ludza. The existing bridge is not in line with the needs of urban people, its technical state and degree of use endangers road safety, making part of the city difficult to reach, thus hindering the implementation of various types of productive and urban development projects in its western part. The project of Ludza Municipality has a high degree of readiness: the reconstruction project of the bridge over the Garbara River Ludza has been developed and accepted in the construction board, and the planned costs have been calculated. The amount will be further specified, but the municipality is prepared to provide part of the necessary construction costs. The beneficiaries of this project will be all, as this will facilitate the establishment of functional links between the development centre and adjacent areas, providing support to entrepreneurs for infrastructure adjustment, creating a favourable environment for business development, and facilitate access to public services and mobility of citizens, which in turn will strengthen the competitiveness of Ludza region as a development centre. I would like to remind you of one fact. This fact is known to all of us. Ludza is the oldest city in Latvia. Next year Ludza will celebrate its 840th anniversary. I think we can help the city build this bridge. Please support the proposal.
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U4540	Thank you. The debate is closed. Is there anything to add on behalf of the Commission?
ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U4541	Yeah. On behalf of the Commission, I would like to add that Mr Jamikov had also come to the Commission's meeting when we looked at this issue. At the Commission meeting, we listened to him. Given that the volume of investment is large enough - over 265 thousand - the Commission has decided that we cannot support it in this draft law. At the same time, however, it was pointed out that for such purposes municipalities could take out a loan from the Treasury to implement infrastructure projects, and this was also recommended to the municipality of Ludza. ( Insight: “What is the possibility?Oh, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, ") On behalf of the Commission, I call for no support.

ParlaMint-LV_2016-11-23-PT12-388-U4729	The “From the heart to Latvia” group voted “no” draft law “On the state budget for 2017” because it continues to administer money, there are no thoughtful investments and support for economic development. Votes against a draft law which, instead of public administration reform and efficiency, increases costs for businesses and households in a hidden way. We believe that the adoption of such a state budget is irresponsible. The draft law “On the State Budget for 2017” does not meet the needs of society, does not in essence solve the growing problems in the field of education and health care, does not create an environment favourable to entrepreneurship. The adoption of the budget turns into a competition by Members about who will build more new stadiums for taxpayers' money, repair roofs, pave park roads and plant trees, issue books or create children's playgrounds. The division of almost EUR 20 million into parliamentary quotas is another testimony to the insatiable appetite of the governing power - increasingly with taxpayers' money to benefit themselves and actually buy voters' voices for themselves and the represented party in municipal elections. If almost 10 million euros were allocated to Members' quotas last year when examining the draft state budget for 2016, then this amount has doubled this year. We call on the public and the law enforcement authorities to carefully assess the relationship between the so-called 'Members' actions and projects supported under quotas with specific political forces and Members and to follow up on how these millions will be used to support next year's municipal election campaigns. The draft laws accompanying the state budget have been drafted in a hurry, in poor quality, and several of them have a very remote connection to the budget. The amended laws show a pitiful reality: officials and politicians look to society and business as potential offenders, not allies. ( J. Reir: “You are also a politician! ” ) The government, when presenting this budget, does not differ from the previous inability to listen to pragmatic entrepreneurs who are prepared to work in our country, pay taxes and earn a living. The government is doing everything possible to destroy a small businessman. The fate of micro-enterprises is not clear, and entrepreneurs are confused about compulsory social security contributions. In the very near future, ignoring the opinion of specialists, without considering that some children will fall out of the education system, boarding schools will be destroyed. But the ruling coalition doesn't care! Although there are 20 million which are divided into quotas, at the same time, the government is not even trying to find a solution to implement the requirements set out in the Law on Higher Education and the Law on Scientific Activities: gradually increasing funding for higher education and science every year. If there is a law, then it must be fulfilled. And above all, Members! But the main thing is ensured: the choirs, dance groups, which enrich the atmosphere of the congresses of the ruling parties, are supported, and next year, when implementing projects in local governments, the leaders of local governments of the ruling parties will also be supported. The “From the heart to Latvia” fraction does not support such short-term thinking and disregarding of public and business opinion. (Applause)
